\subsection{Rebuilding the article and the audit}
From the package directory, run
\begin{lstlisting}
python3 code/verify.py
pdflatex -interaction=nonstopmode -halt-on-error article.tex
pdflatex -interaction=nonstopmode -halt-on-error article.tex
\end{lstlisting}
The first command regenerates the data files.  A smaller main grid is
available with \texttt{--quick}; it is explicitly marked as such in
its output.  The supplied full-run transcript is
\texttt{data/verification\_run.txt}.  The \LaTeX\ source uses ordinary
TeX Live packages, including \texttt{newtxtext} and \texttt{newtxmath}.
The source bibliography is embedded, so no separate bibliography
processor or network access is needed.

\emph{In this report these commands must not be run in place.}  Here
\nolinkurl{code/verify.py} is Part~I's verifier, and the shipped
\nolinkurl{code/03-cyclotomic-verify.py} writes by default into the
report's \nolinkurl{data/}, adding the three unprefixed files
\nolinkurl{verification_summary.json}, \nolinkurl{cyclotomic_orders.json}
and \nolinkurl{orders_c1_c2_c3.csv} beside the shipped ones; the
transcript is its console output, not a file it writes.  The delivered
Makefile, shipped as \nolinkurl{code/03-cyclotomic-Makefile}, has the same
defect when run from the report directory: its \texttt{verify} target runs
Part~I's \nolinkurl{code/verify.py}, which rewrites Part~I's data files;
its \texttt{quick} target passes that
verifier options it does not know, and its \texttt{pdf} and
\texttt{clean} targets build and clean the merged \nolinkurl{article.tex}
in place.  The article is built as described in the report's README.
Restore the delivered layout in a scratch copy instead (POSIX shell, run
from the report directory):
\begin{lstlisting}
W=/path/to/scratch/cyclotomic
mkdir -p "$W/code" "$W/recorded"
cp code/03-cyclotomic-verify.py "$W/code/verify.py"
for f in data/03-cyclotomic-*; do
  cp "$f" "$W/recorded/${f#data/03-cyclotomic-}"; done
cd "$W" && python3 code/verify.py --output data > verification_run.txt
\end{lstlisting}
The run writes three files to \texttt{\$W/data} and the transcript to
\texttt{\$W/verification\_run.txt}; compare them with the copies in
\texttt{\$W/recorded}.  Run this way on 29 September 2026 (Python 3.14.4,
about 30~s), it passed; the CSV was byte-identical and the two JSON files
and the transcript agreed with the recorded ones apart from Windows line
endings.  The quick grid is \texttt{python3 code/verify.py --quick
--output data\_quick}.  The delivery README's example of the root-free
routine, run in the same scratch directory, reproduces
\eqref{cyc:eq:largeexample}:
\begin{lstlisting}
import sys
sys.path.insert(0, "code")
from verify import CyclotomicRing, root_free_factors

ring = CyclotomicRing(12)
c = ring.add(ring.integer(2), ring.add(ring.zp(1), ring.zp(-1)))
L, factors = root_free_factors(8, c, ring)   # c = 2 + sqrt(3)
assert L == 6
assert sum((len(p) - 1) * e for p, e in factors) == 82
\end{lstlisting}
It returns pairs (polynomial coefficients in ascending order, exponent),
and reads neither the spectral generator nor the cyclotomic order.  The
subset expansion in the program is deliberately exponential in~$t$ and
serves testing; the root-free routine is the compact algorithm.

\section{Further research questions and proposed next steps}\label{cyc:sec:future}

The following are precise continuations of the present results.  They
are research tasks relative to this article; they are not all asserted
to be globally unsolved problems in the published literature.

\subsection{Several distinct repeated roots}
For $q(x)=\prod_i(x-c_i)^{t_i}$, equal rates are described by
multiplicative relations among several $z_i$.  The degree function is
$\sum_i k_i(t_i-k_i)$, so maximizing labels lie on a higher-dimensional
lattice rather than a single arithmetic progression.  Can one classify
when several maximal labels cancel, and bound the additional loss in
terms of the dimension or shape of that maximizing set?  The
one-dimensional proof shows exactly which ingredient is missing:
a tractable sum of leading and first surviving coefficients over all
maximal labels.

\subsection{Endpoint factors coupled to a cyclotomic interior root}\label{tec:x:cycendpoint}
Investigate $x^a(x-4)^b(x-c)^t$.  The repository's degree law already
[Theorem~\ref{rc:thm:main}] adds fixed endpoint triangular contributions.  The open structural
issue is how those endpoint blocks alter the ratio of two tied
interior leading coefficients and the first centered correction.
Determine which endpoint multiplicities preserve a one-defect law and
which produce higher-order cancellation.

\subsection{Higher centered coefficients and a symmetric-function formula}
The first correction arose from a logarithmic derivative of a signed
Vandermonde.  Higher corrections involve elementary symmetric
functions of the mixed offsets $i+j+1-t$.  Find a closed differential
or symmetric-function expression for all centered coefficients of
$P_{t,k}$.  A particularly useful target is a formula exposing their
parity under~\eqref{cyc:eq:reflection} and their factors at $z^2=-1$.
The goal is structural information, not merely a larger interpolation.

\subsection{Uniform limits near a disappearing characteristic factor}
The present formulas are exact at fixed parameters.  They do not
provide a uniform large-$N$ approximation as $c$ approaches a
cyclotomic value, especially the center where whole sectors vanish.
Determine the correct scaling of $c-c_0$ with $N$ near a defect and
identify the transition between the generic and specialized
exponential-polynomial expansions.  The exact subset formula is a
finite starting point with no asymptotic error to hide.

\subsection{Degrees and zeros on individual size progressions}
At a cyclotomic parameter, restrict $H_N^{(t)}(c)$ to one residue
class of $N$ modulo a common rate period.  Different characteristic
rates now become the same, so additional cancellations can occur:
\eqref{cyc:eq:c3residues} has a linear progression alongside two quadratic
ones.  Classify the exact degree and leading coefficient on each
progression, and determine which progressions can vanish identically.
This would lead to a precise description of the zero set of the
Hankel determinant at cyclotomic parameters.

\subsection{Other eventually constant Jacobi recurrences}
Replace~\eqref{cyc:eq:prec} by a family with a different finite initial
Jacobi segment and the same constant tail.  The exponential branches
survive, but their amplitudes change.  Determine a condition on those
amplitudes under which only one extra multiplicity can be lost at a
single-root cyclotomic resonance.  Such a theorem would separate the
universal lattice mechanism from the special Catalan boundary
condition.

\subsection{Effective arithmetic and reduction modulo primes}
The root-free algorithm uses exact characteristic-zero equality.
Determine a bit-complexity bound for algebraic inputs, including the
cost of field arithmetic and coefficient growth, and study which
primes preserve the characteristic-zero minimal polynomial on
reduction.  In positive characteristic, finite differences may lower
polynomial degree abnormally and factorial factors may vanish, so
neither the degree lemma nor the minimality theorem transports
unchanged.

\subsection{A focused formalization in Lean}
The shortest reusable formalization targets are the exponential-block
minimality lemma, the nearest-representative and square-sum lemmas,
and the purely algebraic implication from the two sector coefficients
to the one-defect law.  These can be separated from integration and
orthogonal polynomials.  A second stage would formalize the signed
confluent Vandermonde and the Christoffel identity, replacing the
coefficient hypotheses by proved formulas.  This staged plan must
keep imported hypotheses visible rather than labeling the entire
analytic argument formalized after only its final combinatorics has
been checked.

\section{Conclusion}\label{cyc:sec:conclusion}

The single repeated nonendpoint-root problem admits a complete
sign-sensitive answer.  When the rates are distinct, their nonzero
sector leading coefficients give the known cubic generic order.
At a cyclotomic resonance, the parabolic sector-degree function has
at most one tied maximizing pair.  Its leading ratio is a sign; when
that sign is negative, the first centered correction proves that the
loss is exactly one, except at the central root.  The latter is an
entire-group cancellation controlled by Cigler's known product.

This yields the minimal characteristic polynomial at every such
parameter, not merely an annihilating bound.  It also explains why
$t$ and the order of $z^2$ alone do not suffice, counts the exceptional
parameters, and gives a root-free factorization computed directly from
$c$ by two short scalar recurrences.  The proofs and exact audit are
provided for independent checking.  They establish the claims made
here within ordinary mathematics; they do not establish priority or
machine-checked formal status.

\section{Normalization, boundary, and proof audit}\label{cyc:app:audit}

This section is Appendix~A of the manuscript.

\paragraph{Size rather than last index.}
$N$ is the matrix size.  An indexing convention with rows
$0,\ldots,n$ requires $N=n+1$.  The value at $N=0$ is part of the
sequence and equals one.

\paragraph{Multiplier sign.}
The multiplier is $(x-c)^t$, not $(c-x)^t$.
This is the source of $(-1)^{Nt}$ in the Christoffel determinant and
$(-1)^t$ in every characteristic root.  Reversing the multiplier sign
would scale the entire sequence by $(-1)^{Nt}$.

\paragraph{Jet factorials.}
The determinant uses $p_n^{(r)}(c)/r!$.  Accordingly $A_t$ appears
once, in $K_t$, and not a second time in the confluence denominator.
The subset Vandermondes use actual index differences, with their
products of factorials kept explicitly.

\paragraph{Two different orders of a root of unity.}
The theorem uses $L=\ord(z^2)$, while the audit ring uses a primitive
$M$th root $z$, so $L=M/\gcd(M,2)$.  For odd $L$, $z$ can have
order $L$ or $2L$.  These give opposite signs of $z^L$ and can give
different recurrence orders.  This distinction is essential.

\paragraph{Tied constants.}
When $t=L$ and the defect sign is negative, $d_*=0$.
There is no degree $-1$ nonzero polynomial; the whole constant group
is zero and its characteristic exponent is zero.  This case is not
covered by dividing the correction by $d_*$ and must be handled
separately, as in Section~\ref{cyc:sec:proofmain}.

\paragraph{What the computation does not prove.}
Finite success at $t\le12$ does not prove an unbounded-multiplicity
claim.  The general mechanism is the explicit coefficient identity
and the maximizing-pair lemma.  Likewise, inspecting a finite set of
sources does not prove historical novelty.

\paragraph{No unsupported nonvanishing assumptions.}
The Christoffel derivation divides only by a Vandermonde before
confluence, not by weighted Hankel minors.  The central condensation
proof divides by a smaller minor only after its nonzero product
formula has been established by induction.  Minimality uses the
nonzero coefficients actually calculated, not genericity by analogy.

\section{Source and package concordance}\label{cyc:app:concordance}

This section is Appendix~B of the manuscript.

The older root-collisions manuscript is now Part~II of the repository
article cited in Section~\ref{cyc:sec:scope}.  Its standalone Section~12.1
is the cyclotomic-classification question answered here; its
Section~12.2 asks for structural cancellation criteria, addressed here
for the single repeated root.  The generic degree law and finite
recurrence-recovery algorithm belong to that prior work.  Its
certified central fourth-power example is subsumed by the already
known general product.

\noindent[Merge note: the standalone Sections~12.1 and~12.2 are
Sections~\ref{cyc:x:rcq1} and~\ref{cyc:x:rcq2} here; the generic degree
law and the recovery algorithm are Theorems~\ref{rc:thm:main}
and~\ref{rc:thm:finite}; the certified central fourth-power example is
\eqref{rc:eq:centreproduct}.]

The present paper does not claim a general solution of multi-root
resonance, uniform collision asymptotics, or positive-characteristic

\noindent\emph{Part~IV was added on 29 September 2026 in batch~53 of
ProveIt's incoming-report intake, from one manuscript written against the
pinned state of Parts~I--III.  It answers the endpoint half of Part~II's
research question ``Uniform endpoint and root-collision limits''
(Section~\ref{tec:x:rcq4}): for any fixed numbers of roots approaching
$0$ and $4$, with arbitrary collisions inside each cluster, it proves an
exact even convergent expansion of the normalized determinant, its
complete first correction, and a sharp outward scale.  The question's
other half, collisions of roots away from the endpoints, remains open.
Parts~I--III are unchanged except for dated pointers to this addition
(Sections~\ref{sec:status}, \ref{rc:sec:asymptotics}
and~\ref{tec:x:rcq4}), a line on the title page (whose vertical spaces
were reduced by 12\,mm in all so that it still fits on one page) and a
paragraph of the abstract, a contents entry, three bibliography entries used only by
Part~IV (with a label column widened for the longer list), a clause in
the closing note on source dates, and two new labels, on Part~II's
Section~\ref{tec:x:rcq4} and Part~III's Section~\ref{tec:x:cycendpoint}.
Section~\ref{tec:sec:intro} records the provenance and fixes the notation
of the addition.}

\section{Provenance and conventions of Part~IV}\label{tec:sec:intro}

\subsection{One manuscript, one addition}\label{tec:sec:source}
Part~IV is batch-53 manuscript~02, \emph{Two-Endpoint Confluence for
Catalan Hankel Determinants}, subtitled \emph{An exact even finite-size
expansion, the first interaction law, and a sharp critical scale}, an
AI-assisted research article prepared for Vladimir Reshetnikov and dated
29 September 2026 (20 A4 pages as delivered).  Unlike the sources of
Parts~I--III, it does not name the assistant that prepared it.  It
arrived as the archive \nolinkurl{Two_Endpoint_Catalan_Confluence.zip}
(inner directory \nolinkurl{Two_Endpoint_Catalan_Confluence/}, main file
\nolinkurl{article.tex}) in ProveIt commit \texttt{148a906aa} and was
placed in \texttt{1dc874990}.  It was written against ProveIt commit
\texttt{1ee53d57de253d16cdaad79d1f54bbd95d76d682}, reading this report's
\nolinkurl{article.tex} (Git blob
\texttt{e5a4d81408c80f0385ae66c26fbd11bba7dfdb09}) and README through the
GitHub connector; its source notes name Part~II's Sections~22 and~24.4,
and Part~III, as the relevant material.  The report's
\nolinkurl{article.tex} is unchanged between the pin and the placement
commit (the same blob), so every statement the manuscript makes about
``the repository'', ``the source'' or ``the report'' refers to
Parts~I--III as printed above.

The manuscript's program and recorded outputs are shipped beside the
earlier files with the prefix \texttt{04-endpoint-confluence-} (Part~I's
unprefixed files count as~01, Part~II's as~02, Part~III's as~03), its
dependency file under \nolinkurl{data/}, and its source notes and proof
audit, delivered as \nolinkurl{SOURCES.md} and \nolinkurl{PROOF_AUDIT.md},
as \nolinkurl{04-endpoint-confluence-SOURCES.md} and
\nolinkurl{04-endpoint-confluence-PROOF_AUDIT.md} at the report root.  Its
manuscript, delivery README and PDF are not shipped; they survive in the
arrival commit.  Section~\ref{tec:app:archive} lists the shipped files.

The manuscript contributes, in the order printed here: the repository
question, what is classical, and a guide to the proof
(Section~\ref{tec:sec:scope}); the two-endpoint model and its confluent
cosine and sine kernels (Section~\ref{tec:sec:model}); the four main
statements, namely the exact even expansion, the first interaction law, the
endpoint values and the sharp outward scale (Section~\ref{tec:sec:main});
a self-contained Christoffel reduction (Section~\ref{tec:sec:christoffel});
the exact centred hyperbolic alternant that generates every coefficient
(Section~\ref{tec:sec:alternant}); the proof of the expansion, with an
explicit remainder bound (Section~\ref{tec:sec:proofmain}); the derivation
of the first correction (Section~\ref{tec:sec:first}); positive Schur
expansions of the outward profiles (Section~\ref{tec:sec:schur}); the
proof of the sharp scale (Section~\ref{tec:sec:scaleproof}); the answer in
the question's own uncentred scaling, and exact spatial displacements
(Section~\ref{tec:sec:original}); worked examples
(Section~\ref{tec:sec:examples}); its computations and verification
boundary (Section~\ref{tec:sec:verification}); ten research directions
(Section~\ref{tec:sec:future}); a conclusion; a sign and normalization
audit (Section~\ref{tec:app:audit}); and its archive and reproduction
notes (Section~\ref{tec:app:archive}).  Every result, proof, example,
remark, limitation and question of the manuscript is printed.  Where it
re-derives something Parts~I--III already prove, the overlap is marked
(Section~\ref{tec:sec:merge}).

The manuscript's own abstract reads:
\begin{quote}\small
The Catalan-Hankel report in \textit{ProveIt} asks for uniform limits when
a repeated root approaches either endpoint of the Catalan moment interval.
We solve that endpoint problem for arbitrary fixed multiplicities and, more
generally, for two simultaneous clusters with arbitrary internal
collisions. For total multiplier degree $d$, the effective size is
$\nu=N+d/2$. After removing the endpoint sign and the power
$\nu^{\binom m2+\binom{\ell+1}2}$, the determinant is the restriction of a
holomorphic \emph{even} function of $1/\nu$. Its expansion is convergent,
locally uniform in all scaled root coordinates, and has an explicit
Cauchy remainder bound. The leading coefficient factors into two
confluent cosine/sine determinants. We derive the complete first
correction, including a universal mixed derivative with coefficient
$-1/4$. A centered hyperbolic Vandermonde identity generates every higher
coefficient. Positive Schur expansions of the outward profiles then prove
an exact scale criterion: the determinant is asymptotic to its
fixed-endpoint value if and only if all outward displacements are
$o(N^{-2})$; if the largest scaled displacement tends to infinity, the
ratio diverges. Written proofs are accompanied by 810 exact-arithmetic
and high-precision checks, clearly separated by their evidentiary status.
\end{quote}

\begin{center}
\fcolorbox{muted}{lightblue}{\parbox{\dimexpr\textwidth-2\fboxsep-2\fboxrule\relax}{%
\textbf{Status (manuscript~02).}
This is an unrefereed, AI-assisted manuscript with conventional written
proofs, not a Lean or Rocq formalization.  It resolves the endpoint portion
of Part~II's moving-root question, not its nonendpoint collision problem,
and it does not resolve hard-edge universality for arbitrary weights or
limits with multiplicities that grow with~$N$: the multiplicities $m$ and
$\ell$ are fixed, and the constants and the analytic radius are not
claimed to be uniform in them.  The if-and-only-if scale criterion is
restricted to outward, nonnegative displacements.  Classical identities are
credited, not presented as new: the fixed-size Christoffel reduction and its
confluence (Krattenthaler~\cite{Krattenthaler2023}, re-proved in the
present normalization), characteristic-polynomial determinants, integrable
kernels and hard-edge limits from random matrix
theory~\cite{StrahovFyodorov2003,AkemannFyodorov2003}, and the
Mehler--Heine phenomenon of which the one-variable cosine and sine limits
are elementary half-integer cases~\cite{DLMFMehlerHeine}.  The proposed
contribution is the explicit combined package: the simultaneous
two-endpoint, collision-uniform theorem, the centre $N+d/2$, the convergent
expansion without odd powers, the first interaction operator, the
all-orders coefficient generator, the explicit error bound and the sharp
outward criterion.  The literature check was bounded, not exhaustive, and
worldwide priority for every identity or corollary is not established; no
claim of settling a famous external conjecture is made.  The proofs do not
assume the report's sector-degree or resonance theorems.  The 810 recorded
checks (710 exact, 100 at 100 digits) count executions, not universal
theorems; the high-precision checks are diagnostics, not outward-rounded
interval certificates, and the shipped program verifies the leading term
and the first correction; it is not an arbitrary-order coefficient engine.
No proof assistant was run, nothing in Part~IV is formalized, and its
placement beside a Lean project confers no formal status.  (Sources: the
manuscript, its delivery README, and its source notes and proof audit,
\nolinkurl{04-endpoint-confluence-SOURCES.md} and
\nolinkurl{04-endpoint-confluence-PROOF_AUDIT.md}.)}}
\end{center}

\subsection{Reading conventions}\label{tec:sec:reading}
Inside Sections~\ref{tec:sec:scope}--\ref{tec:app:archive}, ``this
article'', ``the present article'' and ``here'' mean manuscript~02 as
printed in Part~IV; ``the repository'', ``the repository's question'',
``the source'' and ``the report'' mean Parts~I--III at the pin
\texttt{1ee53d57d}.  The manuscript cites this report's own section
numbers, which did not change: its ``Part~II, Section~24.4'' is
Section~\ref{tec:x:rcq4} and its ``Section~22'' is
Section~\ref{rc:sec:asymptotics}.  ``The archive'' means the delivered
package, whose program and data are shipped under the prefixed names
listed in Section~\ref{tec:app:archive}.  The manuscript's two appendices
are printed as Sections~\ref{tec:app:audit} and~\ref{tec:app:archive}, so
that Part~I's appendices keep their letters.  Its Sections~1--14 are
Sections~\ref{tec:sec:scope}--\ref{tec:sec:conclusion}, so a manuscript
number $n.j$ of a section, theorem, lemma, corollary or equation is
printed as $(n+44).j$ (for example, its Theorem~3.1 is
Theorem~\ref{tec:thm:main}), and its Appendices~A and~B are
Sections~\ref{tec:app:audit} and~\ref{tec:app:archive}.  The manuscript
cites Krattenthaler's Ramanujan Journal paper through arXiv:2101.04225v5,
pointing to its Theorem~1 and ``the confluent discussion in Section~5'';
that is Part~I's reference~\cite{Krattenthaler2023}.  Its DLMF reference is
Section~18.11(ii), not the Section~18.5 of Part~I's~\cite{DLMF}, and is
listed separately as~\cite{DLMFMehlerHeine}.  Bracketed paragraphs
beginning ``Merge note'' are this merge's, not the manuscript's.

\subsection{Notation of Part~IV}\label{tec:sec:notation}
Part~IV keeps the manuscript's symbols.  Within
Sections~\ref{tec:sec:scope}--\ref{tec:app:archive} every symbol has its
Part~IV meaning, except in merge notes and where a sentence names
Part~I, II or~III or cites one of their equations.  The multiplier has
$m$ roots near $0$ and $\ell$ near $4$ and no other roots; with all of
them at the endpoints it is Part~II's
$q=\gamma x^m(x-4)^\ell\prod_i(x-c_i)^{t_i}$ with $\gamma=1$ and $s=0$,
and $H_N(q)$ is Part~II's \eqref{rc:eq:HN}.
Table~\ref{tec:tab:notation} lists the symbols that a reader of
Parts~I--III is likely to misread.  No symbol was renamed and no
normalization changed.  Two readings need particular care: the letter $t$
is the variable of the kernels in Sections~\ref{tec:sec:model}
to~\ref{tec:sec:scaleproof}, but the multiplicity of one repeated root in
\eqref{tec:eq:question} and Section~\ref{tec:sec:original}, as in
Part~II's question; and the derivative columns of the kernels
\eqref{tec:eq:Km}--\eqref{tec:eq:Kp} are \emph{not} divided by factorials,
whereas repeated-root rows are (Section~\ref{tec:app:audit}).

\subsection{What Part~IV answers, and what stays open}\label{tec:sec:answers}
Part~II's Section~\ref{tec:x:rcq4} asked, for $c_N=2\pm2\cosh(\tau/N)$ and
$q_N=(x-c_N)^t$, for the locally uniform limit of $H_N(q_N)$ normalized by
$(-1)^{tN}N^{t(t+1)/2}$ at the right endpoint and by $N^{t(t-1)/2}$ at the
left, with its value at $\tau=0$, a uniform confluent determinant limit and
error bounds; and, in a second version, for two distinct nonendpoint roots
approaching each other at rate $N^{-1}$.  Part~IV answers the first version.
Corollary~\ref{tec:cor:original} gives the limits $F_t^+(\tau)$ and
$F_t^-(\tau)$, entire and even in $\tau$, their values at $\tau=0$
\eqref{tec:eq:origzero}, and the $N^{-1}$ term, which is only the centring
$N\mapsto N+t/2$; Theorem~\ref{tec:thm:bound} gives an explicit remainder
bound.  It does so inside a larger statement: $m$ roots near $0$ and $\ell$
near $4$ at once, any collision pattern within each cluster, coordinates in
compact complex sets (Theorems~\ref{tec:thm:main} and~\ref{tec:thm:first}),
and a sharp outward scale (Theorem~\ref{tec:thm:scale}).  For $t=1$ the
question noted that the limits are elementary hyperbolic functions; they are
$F_1^-(\tau)=\cosh\tau$ and $F_1^+(\tau)=2\sinh\tau/\tau$
(Section~\ref{tec:sec:examples}).  The second version, collisions away from
the endpoints, is \emph{not} answered: it is Part~IV's first research
question (Section~\ref{tec:x:q1}), and Section~\ref{tec:x:rcq4} stays open
for it.

The same results settle, for endpoint roots, the caveat that closes Part~II's
Section~\ref{rc:sec:asymptotics}: its fixed-parameter estimate cannot be
used with moving parameters, and Part~IV proves the double-scaling limit
directly, on the scale $N^{-2}$, without using that estimate.  They also
treat the limit $a\to0$ that Part~I's Section~\ref{sec:status} names, at
fixed $m$.  For $b\ne0$ and $\varepsilon=a/b$,
$D_N^{(m)}(a,b)=b^NH_N(x^m(x+\varepsilon))$, a cluster of $m+1$ roots at
the endpoint $0$, $m$ of them fixed there.  For real $\varepsilon\ge0$ the
root $-\varepsilon$ is an outward displacement, so by
Theorem~\ref{tec:thm:scale}, with $\ell=0$ and $m+1$ roots,
$D_N^{(m)}(a,b)/D_N^{(m)}(0,b)$ tends to $1$ exactly when
$\varepsilon=o(N^{-2})$, to the profile ratio \eqref{tec:eq:finiteprofile}
when $N^2\varepsilon$ converges, and to infinity when
$N^2\varepsilon\to\infty$.  Joint limits with $m$ growing with $N$ remain
open (Section~\ref{tec:sec:future}, ``Multiplicities growing with
determinant size'').  Part~III's research directions on endpoint factors
coupled to a cyclotomic interior root (Section~\ref{tec:x:cycendpoint}) and
on uniform limits near a disappearing characteristic factor concern
interior roots and are not addressed; Part~IV's second research question
(endpoint clusters in the presence of fixed interior roots) is close to
the first of them.

\emph{Consistency with Parts~II and~III} (observations of this merge,
checked by a separate exact computation not shipped with the report).
\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt]
\item The endpoint value \eqref{tec:eq:Aendpoint} is Part~II's leading
 coefficient \eqref{rc:eq:leading} for $q=x^m(x-4)^\ell$ ($s=0$,
 $\gamma=1$): there $\mathcal W=((-1,m),(1,\ell))$, $\Delta_q=4^{m\ell}$ and
 $\V(\mathcal W)=(-1)^{b_m}2^{m\ell}\prod_{h<m}h!\prod_{h<\ell}h!$, and
 $B_0(m)B_4(\ell)\V(\mathcal W)/\Delta_q$ simplifies to $\mathcal A_{m,\ell}$;
 exact arithmetic agrees for $m,\ell\le3$.  The sign $(-1)^{N\ell}$ removed
 in \eqref{tec:eq:ZN} is Part~II's rate $(-1)^\ell$.  So
 Corollary~\ref{tec:cor:endpoint} is a second route to the case $s=0$ of
 Theorem~\ref{rc:thm:leading}, and it adds the next coefficient.  More is
 true at exact endpoints: Part~II's single sector polynomial $P(N)$ of
 degree $\kappa$ satisfies $\nu^{-\kappa}P=Z(1/\nu)$, and a polynomial in
 $h$ that agrees with the even series $Z(h)$ of Theorem~\ref{tec:thm:main}
 at infinitely many points accumulating at $0$ has only even powers; hence
 $P=\sum_jA_j(\mathbf0,\mathbf0)\nu^{\kappa-2j}$ contains only powers of
 $\nu=N+d/2$ of the parity of $\kappa$.  For example
 $H_N((x-4)^2)=\nu(4\nu^2-1)/3$ with $\nu=N+1$, and
 $H_N(x^2(x-4)^2)=\nu^2(\nu^2-1)/12$ with $\nu=N+2$.
\item The product \eqref{tec:eq:22exact} for $H_N(x^2(x-4)^2)$ is the same
 polynomial as Part~II's \eqref{rc:eq:centreproduct} for $H_N((x-2)^4)$,
 which is Part~III's central product $B_{2,2}(N)$; the coincidence of the
 two determinants is observed (both formulas are proved, and they agree for
 $N\le7$ by direct evaluation), not explained here.
\item The small determinants of Section~\ref{tec:app:audit}
 ($H_N(x)$, $H_N(x-4)$, $H_N(x(x-4))$, $H_N(x^2)$, $H_N((x-4)^2)$) agree with
 direct evaluation for $N\le7$, and the table of
 Section~\ref{tec:sec:examples} agrees with the case \texttt{both-double}
 recorded in \nolinkurl{data/04-endpoint-confluence-verification.json}.
\end{enumerate}

\subsection{Where the merge had to choose}\label{tec:sec:merge}
\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt]
\item \emph{Placement.}  Part~IV is printed after Part~III and before
 Part~I's appendices, so that no section, equation, theorem or table
 number of Parts~I--III moves (checked against the \texttt{.aux} file of a
 build of the committed text).  Its sections continue the numbering
 (Sections~\ref{tec:sec:intro} to~\ref{tec:app:archive}), with the
 concordance of Section~\ref{tec:sec:reading}.

 \texttt{eq:measure}, \texttt{eq:R}, \texttt{eq:M} and \texttt{eq:Q}, would
 have collided with Part~I's bare labels.  Labels were added to the
 manuscript's sixteen sections and to its first research subsection, and

 label was changed.
\item \emph{Overlaps with Parts~I--III.}  The manuscript re-derives, without
 assuming the report correct, several things already proved there.  Printed
 with a pointer and kept: the determinant \eqref{tec:eq:HN} (Part~II's
 \eqref{rc:eq:HN}); the Catalan measure, the monic orthogonal polynomials,
 their trigonometric form \eqref{tec:eq:ptrigplus} and $H_N(1)=1$ (a fourth
 route, after Part~I's Section~\ref{sec:coeffproof}, Part~II's
 Section~\ref{rc:sec:classical}, whose \eqref{rc:eq:ptrig} is
 \eqref{tec:eq:ptrigplus}, and Part~III's
 Section~\ref{cyc:sec:orthogonal}); the Gram integral identity
 \eqref{tec:eq:Andreief} and the Christoffel identity
 (Lemma~\ref{tec:lem:Christoffel} is Part~II's
 Lemma~\ref{rc:lem:Christoffel} for $\gamma=1$, and Part~III's
 Lemma~\ref{cyc:lem:Christoffel} and Part~I's Lemma~\ref{lem:Christoffel}
 are other special cases); the endpoint jets \eqref{tec:eq:endpointjets},
 which are Part~II's \eqref{rc:eq:zerojet} and \eqref{rc:eq:fourjet}
 (the signs $(-1)^{n+r}$ and $(-1)^{n-r}$ agree); and the endpoint value

 Everything else in Part~IV (the evenness and convergence, the first
